# Proposed OEIS comments: A230949 to A233099

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A230949

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A230949>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [3003](../papers/03001-03500/03003-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical for column k:

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical column recurrences are true. Each column counts arrays of a fixed width, and counting those a row at a time gives a transfer matrix, so every column satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most the number of states; checking the ones above is then a finite exact computation, and they hold. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A230964

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A230964>  
Result obtained: **3 September 2026**  
Paper: [2296](../papers/02001-02500/02296-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 7*a(n-1) + 10*a(n-2) + 38*a(n-3) - 132*a(n-4) - 232*a(n-5) - 448*a(n-6) + 320*a(n-7) + 256*a(n-8).
> Empirical g.f.: 2*x^2*(3 - 10*x - 7*x^2 - 58*x^3 + 108*x^4 + 192*x^5 + 64*x^6) / (1 - 7*x - 10*x^2 - 38*x^3 + 132*x^4 + 232*x^5 + 448*x^6 - 320*x^7 - 256*x^8). - _Colin Barker_, Sep 24 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 261 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 8. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A231126>  
Result obtained: **4 September 2026**  
Paper: [739](../papers/00501-01000/00739-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 7*a(n-1) + 8*a(n-2) - 32*a(n-3) - 38*a(n-4) + 40*a(n-5) + 56*a(n-6) - 16*a(n-7) - 16*a(n-8).
> Empirical g.f.: 2*x*(3 - x - 10*x^2 - 12*x^3 + 18*x^4 + 8*x^5 - 8*x^6) / (1 - 7*x - 8*x^2 + 32*x^3 + 38*x^4 - 40*x^5 - 56*x^6 + 16*x^7 + 16*x^8). - _Colin Barker_, Mar 18 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 197 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 8. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A231129

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A231129>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [3943](../papers/03501-04000/03943-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 90 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 191 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 90, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [3395](../papers/03001-03500/03395-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) + a(n-2) - 2*a(n-3) for n>5.
> Empirical g.f.: x*(7 - 6*x - 8*x^2 - 4*x^3 - 8*x^4) / ((1 - x)*(1 + x)*(1 - 2*x)). - _Colin Barker_, Sep 28 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 5. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A231399

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A231399>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [4000](../papers/03501-04000/04000-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 83 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 1594 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 83, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A231705

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A231705>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [189](../papers/00001-00500/00189-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 77 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 190 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 77, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A231954

Paper: [4590](../papers/04501-05000/04590-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 50 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 67 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 50, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrences in the linked file are correct. Column k counts arrays of a fixed width, so it is a walk count in a finite graph and satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence; Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one, its order is the order stated for that column, and so is the order of every tail, so any recurrence of that order is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A232042

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A232042>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3186](../papers/03001-03500/03186-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 4*a(n-1) + 9*a(n-2) -a (n-3) - 6*a(n-4) for n>5.
> Empirical g.f.: x*(4 - x - 16*x^2 - 5*x^3 + 12*x^4) / (1 - 4*x - 9*x^2 + x^3 + 6*x^4). - _Colin Barker_, Oct 02 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 50 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A232152

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A232152>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10340](../papers/10001-10500/10340-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 58 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 153 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 58, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A232582

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A232582>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3403](../papers/03001-03500/03403-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) - a(n-2) + a(n-3) = 2*A005314(n-1).
> Empirical: G.f.: -2*x^2 / ( -1+2*x-x^2+x^3 ). - _R. J. Mathar_, Nov 23 2014

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 90 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A232672

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A232672>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [4514](../papers/04501-05000/04514-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 53 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 87 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 53, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A233085

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A233085>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [2850](../papers/02501-03000/02850-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 49*a(n-1) -450*a(n-2) +1466*a(n-3) -1853*a(n-4) +789*a(n-5) for n>8

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This is true. The array grows sideways, so it has to be counted a column at a time, but the clause about which value turns up first is stated in row major order, and a walk across columns does not read the cells in that order. What the walk can carry is, for each named value, the lowest row it has turned up in so far, together with the order those lowest rows were reached; the clause itself is checked only at the end, since a value's lowest row can still drop later. That makes a(n) a walk count on 199 states, so it satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence and checking the one above is a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 8. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---
